# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

    * `[ ]`   Functional: the EIP-2537 set: `eip2537AddG1` takes two first-group points in the EIP-2537 encoding, 256 bytes, and `eip2537AddG2` two second-group points, 512 bytes, each refusing an operand outside the subgroup and returning the sum, 128 and 256 bytes; `eip2537MulG1` takes a first-group point and a scalar, 160 bytes, and the scalar field's order, and returns the product; `eip2537MsmG1` and `eip2537MsmG2` take a positive multiple of 160 and of 288 bytes, one point and one scalar per term, and the scalar field's order, and return the sum; `eip2537PairingCheck` takes a positive multiple of 384 bytes and returns whether the product of the pairings is one; `eip2537CheckG1` and `eip2537CheckG2` take a point, 128 and 256 bytes, and return when it is in the subgroup and revert otherwise; `eip2537CheckScalar` takes a scalar and the scalar field's order and returns for a scalar below the order and reverts otherwise

* `[ ]`   `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier` **The suite's Solidity delivery verifier, implementing `IDeliveryVerifier`: it verifies a mint statement with its proof and a transfer statement with its proof by recomputing the Rust reference verifier's challenge, equations, and hash-weighted pairing product through `PairingLib`, bit for bit, in the verifier form its constructed precompile set selects, the both-groups form on the `Eip2537` set and the first-group-only form on the `Eip196Eip197` set; one contract constructed with the verifier's precompile set, the scalar field's order, and the generators' encodings, declaring its delivery-statement version and its verifier form; reads the constants that are the same for every cell from the committed constants library and proves itself against the committed mint-proof and transfer-proof vector files of every cell directory**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the registry accepts a credential delivery only when the delivery verifier accepts its proof, and the verifier must accept exactly what `proof/schnorr_fs` accepts, so the contract recomputes the transcript's challenge under the same encoding, tag, and scalar field, the same first messages, and the same weights and pairing product, and refuses every statement and proof the reference refuses (CD-03; CR-09; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and exit for `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier`; the technical requirements' `DeliveryVerifier` entry and the Delivery proof section's verifier statements)
    * `[ ]`   Problem sources: the nodes `proof/schnorr_fs` (the verifier equations in both forms, the weights, the challenge comparison), `proof/mint_statement` and `proof/transfer_statement` (the transcripts' field sequences), `proof/schnorr_fs/challenge` (the challenge as the tagged hash of the transcript's encoding), `hash-to-scalar/keccak256` (the mapping `uint256(keccak256(abi.encodePacked(uint8(tag.length), tag, message))) % r`), `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/constants` (the constants entries), and `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/proof_vectors` (the vector records)

    * `[ ]`   Functional: `verifyTransfer` returns true exactly when the statement's purpose is `PURPOSE_TRANSFER` or `PURPOSE_GRANT` and the transfer relation's challenge check, and in the first-group-only form its hash-weighted pairing product, hold
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a statement whose purpose proves the other relation returns false before any other step; a proof of the other verifier form, recognised by the length of its `secondGroupFirstMessages`, returns false
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a statement member that is not one point of its group's encoded length, a challenge or response that is not below the scalar field order, and `secondGroupFirstMessages` of a length that is neither empty nor three second-group points revert with a custom error, because the reference refuses them at its decoding boundary and never returns a verdict for them
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the contract is constructed with the verifier's precompile set, the scalar field's order, and the generators' encodings, declares the delivery-statement version it verifies through `statementVersion`, and declares its verifier form through `secondGroupArithmetic`, true for the both-groups form
    * `[ ]`   Functional: every mint and transfer vector of each cell directory's committed vector files is accepted by a verifier constructed from that directory's values, and a statement or proof differing from a vector in one context field, one group element, one response, the challenge, or one second-group first message is refused
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the contract holds no storage but its immutable construction values, makes no call but `PairingLib`'s and its own, and holds no constant a generated constants library declares

**Signature services**

* `[ ]`   `signature/ed25519` **Ed25519 signature concrete signing and verifying the complete bytes it is handed over its own public-key and signature types, declaring its scheme identifier, key and signature lengths, recovery support, and layers served; creates the `adapters/signature` crate and authors the signature family's generic interface, layer enum, declaration, and mock**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: signatures at the handshake and content-signing layers are produced and verified through one repo-owned interface, each adapter's key and signature types are its own, every adapter declares its scheme identifier, key and signature lengths, whether it recovers a signer's key, and the layers it serves, and no module outside a concrete names the Ed25519 library (CR-03; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Signature Adapter statement; the system architecture's `ISignatureAdapter` signatures; the dependency map's `signature/ed25519` row)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's generic interface signs a borrowed message under a borrowed secret key and returns the signature in the adapter's own signature type
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's generic interface reports whether a signature is valid for a message under a public key, over the adapter's own public-key and signature types, and the answer is a boolean in the success arm
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete signs and verifies the complete message bytes it is handed, with no prehash and no truncation, so a signature over one byte sequence is invalid for every other byte sequence, a longer one and a prefix included (CR-03 complete-message binding, with the domain separation inside the bytes the caller hands it, since the signature family owns no message format)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete verifies strictly, so a signature the strict check refuses is invalid
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete declares the scheme identifier as the domain's `SignatureSchemeIdentifier`, the public-key length, the signature length, that it does not recover a signer's key, and the layers it serves, the handshake and content-signing layers, with its adapter version and the interface version it implements
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete's public-key and signature types are built only through fallible constructors that refuse a byte length other than the scheme's, and the public-key constructor also refuses bytes that are not a curve point, with the vendor's refusal carried unchanged; both types expose their bytes through an accessor
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete's output for the specification's known-answer inputs equals RFC 8032's published signatures
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: `ed25519-dalek` is named only inside `adapters/signature/src/ed25519`; the secret key's bytes are zeroized after every signing; no key is recovered and no address is derived

    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete's public-key and signature types are built only through fallible constructors, the public key admitting only the 33-byte compressed SEC1 encoding of a curve point and the signature only 65 bytes the vendor admits, each carrying the vendor's refusal unchanged, and both types expose their bytes through an accessor

**Swarm transport, discovery, storage and seed host**

* `[ ]`   `transport/rqbit` **BitTorrent transport concrete over embedded `librqbit` on the overlay branch the workspace manifest names: translates an object's Bao root to the BitTorrent info-hash locator by creating one torrent per object, serves published objects, retrieves an object by locator while Bao-verifying every completed piece after the library's SHA-1 check and before the piece counts as held, injects peers into a managed torrent, releases a served object, and exposes the library's DHT, tracker, and peer-exchange peer feeds as the engine capability; creates the `adapters/transport` crate and authors the transport family's generic interface, locator types, declaration, configuration, engine capability, and mock**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: ciphertext and sidecar objects move between participants by authenticated root over one repo-owned transport interface; the adapter, not the library, owns the translation from an object's root to the transport's identifier; every piece the library hands back is verified against the object's Bao root after the library's own check and before it is held; failures leave nothing unauthenticated at the destination; the library's discovery feeds reach the discovery family only through a capability the transport's generic surface exposes; and no module outside the concrete names the BitTorrent library (SW-01, SW-06, EC-02; `docs/research/MVP Application Requirements.md`'s SW-01, SW-06, and EC-02 rows; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Swarm Transport and Two integrity structures where a transport requires it statements; the product requirements' statement that the MVP's sole swarm transport is BitTorrent through embedded `librqbit` and its list of what the concrete owns; the dependency map's `transport/rqbit` row; the Swarm transport and seed host milestone's scope for `transport/rqbit`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: publishing an object verifies the file's bytes against the object's root and outboard through the hashing family, creates a single-file torrent named by the root's lowercase hexadecimal text over the piece length the payload carries, a multiple of the hashing adapter's chunk length, begins serving it, and returns the object's locator; the same bytes always yield the same locator
    * `[ ]`   Functional: retrieving an object adds the torrent by the locator's info-hash, takes peers the caller injects, stages the bytes under a caller-named staging directory, rejects every piece, at the piece length the payload carries, that fails the Bao check so the library requests it again, and moves the staged file to the caller-named destination only after the whole staged file verifies against the root and outboard; within the deadline it returns the length held, and past it returns the count of pieces rejected
    * `[ ]`   Functional: releasing an object stops the library serving it without deleting the destination's bytes; injecting peers adds addresses to a managed object's peer set and reports how many the library had not seen
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the engine capability returns the peers one declared feed yields for a locator within a deadline, and the declaration lists the feeds the concrete exposes
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete hands the family configuration's rate limits, peer transports, port mapping, announce port, bind interface, IP family, per-object peer limit, proxy, and blocklist to the library's session options as the family configuration states them, so each setting the family configuration carries is a setting the session runs under
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete declares its transport identifier, that its wire integrity is the legacy piece hashes carried beside the Bao check, the engine feeds it exposes, its adapter version, and the interface version it implements
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the locator type is built only through a fallible constructor that refuses a length other than twenty bytes and the all-zero value, and renders the info-hash text and the standard magnet link the canonical record exposes
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: `librqbit` is named only inside `adapters/transport/src/rqbit`; every value the concrete uses to choose a listen address, an enabled feed, a tracker, a rate limit, a peer transport, a port mapping, an announce port, a bind interface, an IP family, a peer limit, a proxy, or a blocklist comes from the family configuration its constructor params carry, the piece length comes from the payload of the call that places the object, and its session directory comes from its own configuration; the hashing concrete is reached only through `create_commitment`

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Adapter: the transport family's first concrete, and the first source file that requires the family's generic interface, locator types, declaration, configuration, engine capability, and mock, which it authors in the family's `factory` module as its producers
    * `[ ]`   Creates `factory/mod.rs` with the module wiring and the locator type's constructor and accessors, and `factory/provides.rs` with the interface and mock surface; the factory function, its types, interaction spec, unit test, re-export, and the family's integration test are `transport/factory`'s
    * `[ ]`   Does not read a hash-card or a deployment record; the caller hands the root, outboard, locator, paths, and piece length
    * `[ ]`   Does not decide holding reason, quota, or eviction, or where a destination lives; the caller names the staging directory and the destination
    * `[ ]`   Does not discover peers on its own initiative; discovery concretes call the engine capability and the caller injects the peers it aggregates
    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit; the milestone's integration test and commit are carried by `seed-host/factory`

    * `[ ]`   Sources: SW-02, whose row in `docs/research/MVP Application Requirements.md` requires multiple discovery implementations to run concurrently, union and deduplicate results, and treat no discovery source as authoritative, and whose proof returns overlapping, conflicting, unavailable, and malicious discovery results; the technical requirements' peer discovery family row, whose factory owns `IPeerDiscoveryAdapter` and the peer types, and its file-tree rule that a function a family owns is a module directory addressed `crate/function`; the dependency map's statements that a family's first ticket, a concrete or a family-owned function preceding it, authors the generic interface, the declaration, and the family's mock as its producers and creates the crate, and that a function a family owns is `crate/function`

  * `[ ]`   `adapters/discovery/src/admit_peers/interaction.spec.md`
    * `[ ]`   The title `` # `admit_peers` — interaction spec `` and one opening sentence stating the file as the branch contract for the `admit_peers` function of the `discovery` crate; then one `##` section headed by the full signature `admit_peers(deps: &AdmitPeersDeps, params: AdmitPeersParams, payload: AdmitPeersPayload) -> AdmitPeersReturn`, the trusted form, then `## Ordering and edges`, then `## Integration`
    * `[ ]`   Per address in `payload.addresses`, in order, refused: condition `PeerAddress::try_new(PeerAddressConstructorParams { address })` returns `Err`; decision that result; dependency call `PeerAddress::try_new`, exactly once per address; outcome the address is dropped
    * `[ ]`   Per address, repeated: condition the admitted `PeerAddress` equals the address of a peer already kept; decision equality; outcome the repeat is dropped
    * `[ ]`   Per address, admitted: condition the constructor returns `Ok(address)` and the address is not already kept; outcome `DiscoveredPeer { address, source: params.source }` appended, so the result is in first-arrival order
    * `[ ]`   Return: outcome `Ok(AdmitPeersSuccessReturn { peers })`, empty when no address is admitted; the error arm is uninhabited, so callers bind the success arm irrefutably

* `[ ]`   `discovery/local` **Local-network discovery concrete over the BEP 14 Local Service Discovery implementation `librqbit-lsd`: announces the port a participant serves an object on to the local network and returns the peers that answer or announce the same object key within a deadline, each address admitted through the family's `admit_peers`**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: a participant finds peers for an object on its own local network without any service, by announcing and listening on the multicast groups BEP 14 names; the results are data from the network and so are untrusted, and no address the peer type refuses reaches a consumer; the source is one among several concurrent, non-authoritative sources, so its results carry the source that produced them; and no module outside the concrete names the Local Service Discovery library (SW-02; `docs/research/MVP Application Requirements.md`'s SW-02 row; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`'s Swarm Transport and Peer Discovery statement that local discovery is one peer discovery adapter among several, none authoritative; the technical requirements' peer discovery family row and file tree entry; the system architecture's `discovery` row and its statement that a transport is admissible only if it can carry what discovery advertises; the dependency map's `discovery/local` row; the Swarm transport and seed host milestone's scope for `discovery/local`; BEP 14, Local Service Discovery, external context, for the multicast groups, the `BT-SEARCH` message, the announce interval, and the receiver's reading of the sender's address)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `discover` announces the participant's serving port for the key when the payload carries one, listens for announces of the same key from other participants for the params' deadline, and returns the addresses it heard, each admitted by `admit_peers` with `DiscoveryIdentifier::LocalServiceDiscoveryV1` as its source, in first-arrival order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: an adapter never returns its own announce; another adapter on the same network announcing the same key is returned, an announce of another key is not
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the concrete declares its discovery identifier, the key kinds it can look up, its adapter version, and the interface version it implements
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: `librqbit-lsd` and `librqbit-core` are named only inside `adapters/discovery/src/local`; the multicast groups, port, announce interval, and the self-announce filter are the library's, none restated in this crate; the deadline comes from the call's params and the serving port from its payload; dropping the adapter stops the library's receive task

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Adapter: a concrete of the discovery family, implementing the family's trait
    * `[ ]`   Does not read a hash-card, a deployment record, or a locator of any transport; the caller hands the key it has built from the transport's locator
    * `[ ]`   Does not aggregate, deduplicate across sources, or decide which source to trust; it deduplicates only the addresses of its own call
    * `[ ]`   Does not inject peers into a transport; the caller passes the peers it aggregates to the transport
    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit; the milestone's integration test and commit are carried by `seed-host/factory`

  * `[ ]`   `module`
    * `[ ]`   Bounded context: the `adapters/discovery` crate's private `local` concrete, holding the adapter over `librqbit-lsd`, its constructor params and errors, and the call to `admit_peers` for heard addresses
    * `[ ]`   Outside: the transport's locator, the conversion of a locator to a key, the engine capability the library-backed concretes read, the aggregation of results across sources, the factory's selection of a concrete, and the transport's own use of the library's Local Service Discovery, which `transport/rqbit` disables so that this concrete is the only local source
